We want a small service, Ledger, that keeps every customer\n8\taccount's balance current from PayCo's webhooks.\n9\t\n10\t## What PayCo sends\n11\t\n12\tPayCo sends an HTTP POST to our webhook URL for every event on our merchant account. It treats any 2xx as \"delivered\".\n39\t- **Ordering.** PayCo does not guarantee delivery order. A refund can arrive before the payment it\n40\t  refers to.\n41\t- **Duplicates.** PayCo may deliver the same event more than once, even after it received a 2xx.\n42\t\n43\t## Our side\n44\t\n45\t- The database is a managed network database. Writes occasionally fail partway through a request\n46\t  (our client raises `StoreError`). The failure rate is low but not zero.\n47\t- Finance reconciles every account against PayCo's monthly statement, and the two must match\n48\t  exactly, to the minor unit.\n49\t- Each customer account holds a single currency, fixed by its first payment.\n50\t- Security: if we ever receive the same event `id` with a different body, treat it as an\n51\t  incident: reject that delivery and leave the account untouched.\n52\t- Volume is small today (a few thousand events a day) and expected to grow.\n53\t\n54\t## What we need\n55\t\n56\tA Python service, standard library only, with this interface:\n57\t\n58\t```\n59\tLedgerService(store, secret, clock)\n60\t    store:  our database client, a ledgerkit MemoryStore (see README.md)\n61\t    secret: the webhook signing secret, bytes\n62\t    clock:  an object whose now() returns the current Unix time in seconds\n63\t\n64\thandle(raw_body: bytes, headers: dict) -> (status: int, body: dict)\n65\t    Processes one webhook delivery. Customers top up through our payment provider, PayCo, and spend the\ncredit in our product. Today a nightly script copies PayCo's CSV export into a spreadsheet and\nsupport staff fix balances by hand. We want a small service, Ledger, that keeps every customer\naccount's balance current from PayCo's webhooks.\n\n## What PayCo sends\n\nPayCo sends an HTTP POST to our webhook URL for every event on our merchant account. The body is\nJSON, for example:\n\n```json\n{\"id\": \"evt_1Nq3xKp\", \"type\": \"payment.succeeded\", \"created\": 1700000000,\n \"data\": {\"account_id\": \"acct_42\", \"payment_id\": \"pay_9xk\", \"amount\": 2500, \"currency\": \"EUR\"}}\n```\n\n- `id` identifies the event. A redelivery of the same event has the same `id` and the same body.\n- `type` is one of:\n  - `payment.succeeded`: the customer paid `amount`; credit it to `account_id`.\n  - `refund.succeeded`: PayCo refunded the payment `payment_id` in full, and `amount` equals that\n    payment's amount; take it back from `account_id`.\n  - Other types: PayCo adds new event types from time to time (disputes, payouts, ...). We do not\n    handle any of them yet; we will add them later.\n- `created` is when the event happened, in Unix seconds.\n- `amount` is an integer number of minor units (cents). `currency` is an ISO 4217 code.\n\n## How PayCo delivers (from PayCo's integration guide)\n\n- **Signatures.** Every delivery carries the header `PayCo-Signature: t=<unix seconds>,v1=<hex>`,\n  where `<hex>` is the lowercase hex HMAC-SHA256 of the string `<t>.` followed by the exact request\n  body bytes, keyed with our webhook secret. The guide says to reject a delivery whose signature\n  does not verify, and one whose `t` is more than 300 seconds away from our clock, so that captured\n  requests cannot be replayed.\n- **Retries.** If our endpoint does not answer with a 2xx status, PayCo retries the delivery with\n  backoff for up to 72 hours. It treats any 2xx as \"delivered\".\n- **Ordering.** PayCo does not guarantee delivery order. A refund can arrive before the payment it\n  refers to.\n- **Duplicates.** PayCo may deliver the same event more than once, even after it received a 2xx.\n\n## Our side\n\n- The database is a managed network database. Writes occasionally fail partway through a request\n  (our client raises `StoreError`). The failure rate is low but not zero.\n- Finance reconciles every account against PayCo's monthly statement, and the two must match\n  exactly, to the minor unit.\n- Each customer account holds a single currency, fixed by its first payment.\n- Security: if we ever receive the same event `id` with a different body, treat it as an\n  incident: reject that delivery and leave the account untouched.\n- Volume is small today (a few thousand events a day) and expected to grow.\n\n## What we need\n\nA Python service, standard library only, with this interface:\n\n```\nLedgerService(store, secret, clock)\n    store:  our database client, a ledgerkit MemoryStore (see README.md)\n    secret: the webhook signing secret, bytes\n    clock:  an object whose now() returns the current Unix time in seconds\n\nhandle(raw_body: bytes, headers: dict) -> (status: int, body: dict)\n    Processes one webhook delivery. Header names may arrive in any letter case. Our HTTP layer\n    returns `status` to PayCo as the response code.\n\nbalance(account_id) -> int\n    The account's current balance in minor units; 0 for an account we have never seen.\n\nentries(account_id) -> list of dict\n    The money movements applied to the account, oldest first. Each has at least `event_id`,\n    `type` (the event's type, e.g. `payment.succeeded`), and `amount` (signed: positive for\n    payments, negative for refunds).\n```\n\nThe HTTP server, deployment, and monitoring are outside this codebase; a plan may still cover\nthem.\n","numLines":79,"startLine":1,"totalLines":79}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_bdrk_01JagWEMfgNXfTfkmRQmVs2C","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# BUILD PLAN: Ledger (customer balances from PayCo webhooks)\n2\t\n3\t## 1. Classification and constraints\n4\t- **What:** A Python (standard library only) `LedgerService` that verifies PayCo webhooks and keeps per-account balances and entries from `payment.succeeded` and `refund.succeeded`. It replaces a nightly CSV-to-spreadsheet script and manual balance fixes.\n5\t- **Type:** Software. Brownfield: the spreadsheet is the system of record today. Not a small build, because it handles money and takes input from an outside party.\n6\t- **Dominant constraint:** Correctness. Balances must match PayCo's statement exactly, to the minor unit.\n7\t- **Worst failure:** An account gets credit PayCo never collected, through a forged event or a payment applied twice after a duplicate delivery or a partial write. The customer spends it, and nobody notices until the monthly reconciliation.\n8\t\n9\t**Budgets**\n10\t\n11\t| Budget | Target | Label | Checked by |\n12\t|---|---|---|---|\n13\t| Correctness | 0 minor-unit difference per account vs PayCo statement | REQUIREMENT (brief: Finance reconciliation) | V3, V5 |\n14\t| Replay window | Reject if \\|now − t\\| > 300 s | REQUIREMENT (PayCo integration guide) | V2, V4 |\n15\t| RPO for acknowledged events | 0: no 2xx until the outcome is committed durably | REQUIREMENT (derived: PayCo treats any 2xx as delivered and will not reliably resend) | V1 |\n16\t| RTO | ≤ 4 h | ASSUMPTION. PayCo retries for 72 h (REQUIREMENT ceiling), so 4 h leaves about 68 h of headroom | Phase 3 restore drill |\n17\t| Latency | p99 `handle` below PayCo's delivery timeout | UNKNOWN: PayCo's timeout (PayCo docs or support; needed in Phase 2 for the sync/async row). Interim p99 ≤ 1 s (ASSUMPTION) | V0 baseline, Phase 3 exit |\n18\t| Throughput | ~5k events/day now; design check at 10× (50k/day) | ASSUMPTION (brief says \"a few thousand\"; confirm against PayCo export counts in Phase 0). Growth rate UNKNOWN (product owner) | Phase 3 exit |\n19\t| Drift detection window | ≤ 1 day (monthly today) | ASSUMPTION (a daily diff against PayCo's daily export is cheap) | Phase 3, V5 |\n20\t| Storage | ≤ 10 MB/day of raw event bodies | ASSUMPTION (~1–2 KB/event × 5k) | Phase 3 |\n21\t| Operational complexity | One process plus the managed DB; no queue, no cache | ASSUMPTION (team size not given) | Design |\n22\t\n23\t**Missing inputs** (the ones that would change the plan most):\n24\t1. **ledgerkit README / MemoryStore API.** It is not in the workspace. I need to know whether the store has put-if-absent, compare-and-set, multi-key transactions or prefix scans, and whether a `StoreError` can be raised *after* the write took effect. These answers decide the data model (V1). The dev lead supplies them before Phase 1.\n25\t2. **Does Ledger also record spends?** I assume not: Ledger holds funded credit (payments minus refunds), and spending is recorded elsewhere. If Ledger must take debits, a debit API and its contract become a new phase before Phase 3.\n26\t3. **PayCo facts:** whether retries are re-signed with a fresh `t` (V4), the delivery timeout, whether a test mode exists, which fields the historical export has, and whether secret rotation is supported.\n27\t4. **Finance facts:** the statement format (gross or net, fees), what the manual spreadsheet fixes represent, and how unhandled event types (disputes) should be treated in reconciliation. These are needed by Phase 3.\n28\t5. **Named people:** a security reviewer (V2), the Finance approver (V5, V6), and the on-call owner for incident alerts.\n29\t6. **The meaning of \"first payment\"** for the currency rule. I assume the first payment *applied*, not the earliest by `created`.\n30\t7. **What depends on the spreadsheet.** The nightly script and support staff are CONFIRMED by the brief. That the product reads balances from the spreadsheet is ASSUMED; Phase 0 confirms it.\n31\t\n32\t## 2. Dependency map\n33\t\n34\t| What waits | Depends on | Kind | Blocked from being |\n35\t|---|---|---|---|\n36\t| Data model and commit design (Phase 1) | MemoryStore API docs (missing input 1) | organizational / decision | specified |\n37\t| Data model hardening, and everything built on it | V1 (single-commit-point atomicity under `StoreError`) | validation | hardened |\n38\t| Final timestamp-tolerance design | V4 (PayCo re-signs retries) | validation | specified |\n39\t| Live PayCo traffic into Ledger (Phase 3) | V2 (signature, replay and conflict controls) and V3 (order/duplicate/fault correctness) | risk-security | exposed |\n40\t| Webhook registration and signing secret | PayCo dashboard admin access | organizational | deployed (Phase 0) |\n41\t| V4, and the skeleton's real request | PayCo test mode, or else live events in shadow | organizational | Phase 0 |\n42\t| Backfill of historical payments (Phase 3) | PayCo historical export with `payment_id` (data-access approval) | organizational | built |\n43\t| Reconciliation spec (Phase 3) | Finance sample statement and its gross/net rules (starts Phase 0) | organizational | specified |\n44\t| Product or support reading Ledger balances (Phase 4) | V5 plus Finance sign-off | validation / risk-security | exposed |\n45\t| Retiring the nightly script and spreadsheet | V6 plus a named Finance approver | risk-security / organizational | committed |\n46\t| Running more than one writer instance | Compare-and-set primitive (V1 result) | decision | deployed (deferred) |\n47\t\n48\tNo economic dependency binds: a few thousand events a day is far below any store or compute limit. Brownfield dependents of the spreadsheet: the nightly script and support staff are CONFIRMED; the product reading balances is ASSUMED.\n49\t\n50\t## 3. Tradeoff gates (resolved up front)\n51\t\n52\t| Decision | Reversibility | Default (chosen now) | Assumption | Validated by | Flip condition |\n53\t|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n54\t| Consistency vs availability | R3: a balance that is wrong even briefly can be spent, and that loss is not recoverable | Consistent: no 2xx until committed; `StoreError` returns 503 and PayCo's retry does the recovery | Store outages are much shorter than 72 h | Cited basis: PayCo's 72 h retry vs a 4 h RTO (ASSUMPTION) | Store outages over 24 h observed → add a durable local inbox in front of the store |\n55\t| Ledger data model | R3: reconciliation and every later handler rest on it once real data exists | Append-only: one entry per event; **one conditional write is the commit point** for each event's outcome; balance is the sum of entries; every verified raw body is kept in an inbox keyed by event `id` | The store offers put-if-absent or an atomic multi-key write; a `StoreError` may hide a write that succeeded | V1 | V1 fails → a per-account single-key log with version compare-and-set; if the store has neither primitive, stop and ask for a transactional store |\n56\t| Unknown event types | R3: answering 2xx without storing the event loses it for good | Verify, store raw in the inbox, return 200 `{\"status\":\"ignored\"}`, replay from the inbox when a handler ships | PayCo stops redelivering after a 2xx | Cited basis: PayCo retry semantics in the guide | Inbox storage cost exceeds budget → keep raw bodies only for types we expect to handle |\n57\t| Timestamp tolerance vs 72 h retries | R3: rejecting valid retries loses money events permanently | ±300 s, as the guide says | Each PayCo retry is re-signed with a fresh `t` | V4 | V4 fails → escalate to PayCo; accept a stale `t` only for an `id` already in the inbox (idempotency makes that replay harmless); recover the rest through the daily reconciliation |\n58\t| Sync vs async processing | R2: weeks to put a queue in front | Synchronous: verify → commit → respond inside the request | Commit p99 is well under PayCo's timeout (UNKNOWN) | V0 baseline plus the Phase 3 latency check | p99 above 50 % of PayCo's timeout → store in the inbox first, return 2xx, apply asynchronously |\n59\t| Refund arriving before its payment | R2: changes stored state and entry order | **Park** the refund (return 200 `parked`) and apply it right after its payment commits. Then check account, amount and currency, and allow at most one refund per `payment_id` | Payments arrive within the retry window, or are in the backfill | V3, plus the Phase 3 metric for parked-refund age | A parked refund whose payment never arrives shows up in shadow → close the backfill gap, or agree a negative-balance policy with Finance |\n60\t| Writer concurrency | R2: moving to multiple instances needs a compare-and-set commit | One process with a per-account lock; a sweep releases parked refunds after every payment commit and on a timer | 5k events/day fits easily in one process; PayCo's retries cover downtime | V1 (whether compare-and-set exists) and the Phase 3 load check | Need for high availability or more throughput → a compare-and-set based commit and multiple instances |\n61\t| Data-privacy boundary | R2: redacting stored bodies later means a data migration | Raw bodies only in a restricted inbox table; logs carry `event_id`, `account_id`, type, amount and outcome, and never the body, signature or secret; out of PCI scope (PayCo holds card data) | Payloads carry no card numbers and no personal data beyond `account_id` | Phase 3 scan of the stored inbox against PayCo's event catalogue | Personal data found (for example dispute evidence) → field-level redaction before storage, plus a retention policy |\n62\t| System of record and cutover | R3: balances customers see, and Finance's records | The spreadsheet stays system of record. Ledger runs in shadow, data flows one way (PayCo → Ledger), then cuts over by cohort; the nightly script stays runnable until V6 | Shadow diffs show where Ledger is wrong | V5, V6 | V5 fails → stay in shadow |\n63\t| Opening balances | R2: re-backfilling is days of work | Backfill historical payments and refunds from PayCo's export, keyed by `payment_id`. Do not import the hand-fixed spreadsheet | The export has `payment_id`, `account_id`, amount, currency and the refund link | V5 (backfilled totals vs statement) | The export lacks ids → one opening-balance entry per account from a Finance-signed statement, plus a lookup table for refunds of older payments |\n64\t\n65\t**R1 defaults:**\n66\t- Monolith vs services → one library class in one process.\n67\t- Build vs buy → build (REQUIREMENT: standard library only).\n68\t- Body comparison for conflicts → SHA-256 of canonical JSON (sorted keys, compact form). The raw-bytes hash is also stored for forensics.\n69\t- Entry order → commit sequence, with `created` included in each entry.\n70\t- Body conflict → 409, write an incident record, alert; the account is not touched (REQUIREMENT).\n71\t- Anomalies (currency mismatch, refund amount/account/currency mismatch, second refund or duplicate `payment_id` under a new `id`, a schema violation in a signed body) → keep them in quarantine, never apply them, return 200 `quarantined`, page on-call.\n72\t\n73\t**N/A:** Tenancy model (one merchant account); AI rows (no AI component).\n74\t\n75\t## 4. Walking skeleton (Phase 0)\n76\t- **Real request:** a PayCo test-mode `payment.succeeded` for 2500 EUR → 200 `{\"status\":\"applied\",\"event_id\":…}`. Then an internal `ledger balance acct_test` returns 2500. If PayCo has no test mode, the skeleton uses one live event in shadow.\n77\t- **Tiers:** PayCo → TLS ingress → standard-library HTTP adapter (passes the raw bytes and headers through untouched) → `LedgerService.handle` → managed DB in a shadow namespace → response. Plus an internal read-only CLI that calls `balance` and `entries`.\n78\t- **Already present in the skeleton:** a case-insensitive header lookup; signature parsing; the time check uses the injected clock; HMAC over the exact header `t` string plus `.` plus the raw bytes, compared with `hmac.compare_digest`; the signature is checked *before* the JSON is parsed. Event handling is naive: payments only, no deduplication yet. Not hardened.\n79\t- **Deployed:** the CI pipeline runs tests and builds a versioned artifact with a pinned Python version, then deploys to production.\n80\t- **Logged:** one JSON log line per delivery: request id, `event_id`, type, status, outcome, latency, clock skew (now − t). No body, no signature.\n81\t- **Monitored:** alerts on 5xx rate, 401 rate and `StoreError` count.\n82\t- **Rolled back:** redeploy the previous artifact. The kill switch is disabling the endpoint in PayCo; the 72 h retry buffer means nothing is lost.\n83\t- **Who can reach it:** the URL has to be public for PayCo to reach it, but only verified requests have any effect. It receives test-mode events only, writes only to the shadow namespace, and nothing reads it (not the product, not support).\n84\t\n85\tExit check (V0): see §6.\n86\t\n87\t## 5. Phases\n88\t\n89\t**Phase 1: Ledger core (data model and idempotency)**\n90\t- **Unlocks:** every event handler, and live shadow traffic.\n91\t- **Depends on:** V0; MemoryStore API docs.\n92\t- **Tasks:**\n93\t  1. Read the MemoryStore API.\n94\t  2. Build a `FaultyStore` wrapper that raises `StoreError` before, or after, the Nth write.\n95\t  3. Run V1 against six scenario scripts: payment, duplicate, refund, refund-first, unknown type, conflict.\n96\t  4. Specify the records:\n97\t     - inbox (`id` → raw body, canonical hash, raw hash, received time)\n98\t     - outcome or entry (`id` → applied, ignored, parked or quarantined, plus the signed amount); this is the single commit write\n99\t     - `payment_id` uniqueness for payments and for refunds\n100\t  5. Implement the flow in this order: signature → JSON parse → inbox put-if-absent with a body comparison (a different body gives 409 plus an incident) → **\"duplicate\" is decided from the outcome record, never from the inbox record alone.** An inbox hit without an outcome means a retry after a partial failure, so roll forward. Then commit and respond.\n101\t  6. Map any `StoreError` to 503 with no partial effect visible.\n102\t  7. `balance` is the sum of committed entries, 0 for an unknown account; `entries` is in commit order, `[]` for an unknown account.\n103\t  8. Unknown types are stored and return 200 `ignored`.\n104\t- **Rollback:** shadow data only. Wipe the namespace and redeploy the Phase 0 artifact.\n105\t- **Exit check:** V1 passes. An unsigned request carrying an existing `id` gets 401 and never touches the inbox, so nobody can plant a fake body under a real event `id`.\n106\t\n107\t**Phase 2: Money semantics**\n108\t- **Unlocks:** correct balances for both handled types; readiness for live traffic.\n109\t- **Depends on:** Phase 1, V1.\n110\t- **Tasks:**\n111\t  1. Strict validation:\n112\t     - `amount` is an `int`, not a `bool` or `float`, and > 0\n113\t     - `currency` matches `^[A-Z]{3}$`\n114\t     - ids are non-empty strings; `created` is an `int`\n115\t     - a signed body that fails validation goes to quarantine\n116\t  2. Currency is fixed by the first applied payment; a payment in another currency goes to quarantine.\n117\t  3. Refund handling:\n118\t     - payment not yet seen → park\n119\t     - payment seen → check account, amount and currency, allow one refund per `payment_id`, then apply a negative entry\n120\t     - otherwise → quarantine\n121\t     - a refund of a quarantined payment → quarantine\n122\t  4. Release parked refunds when the payment commits (roll forward if interrupted), plus a timed sweep; alert when a refund has been parked longer than 96 h.\n123\t  5. Reprocess tool: re-runs ignored or quarantined inbox events after a code fix, idempotently.\n124\t  6. Parse multiple `v1` values in the signature header.\n125\t  7. Run V3, run V2 and get the reviewer's sign-off, and finish V4 (it started in Phase 0).\n126\t- **Rollback:** redeploy the Phase 1 artifact; rebuild the shadow state from the inbox.\n127\t- **Exit check:** V2, V3 and V4 pass.\n128\t\n129\t**Phase 3: Live shadow, backfill and reconciliation** (system of record is still the spreadsheet; data flows one way, PayCo → Ledger)\n130\t- **Unlocks:** evidence for the cutover.\n131\t- **Depends on:** V2, V3, V4; the PayCo export; the Finance statement sample.\n132\t- **Tasks:**\n133\t  1. Register the live endpoint with a live secret.\n134\t  2. Run the backfill from PayCo's export, keyed by `payment_id`, so it is idempotent and webhook duplicates of the same events collapse onto it.\n135\t  3. Daily reconciliation job: Ledger vs PayCo's daily export (the one the nightly script already pulls) and vs the spreadsheet, per account, with differences sorted into categories.\n136\t  4. Monthly reconciliation against the statement, with Finance.\n137\t  5. Restore drill to measure RTO.\n138\t  6. Replay 10× volume from the inbox against a staging copy to check latency and throughput.\n139\t  7. Scan the stored inbox for personal data.\n140\t  8. Run V5.\n141\t- **Rollback:** disable the live endpoint. Ledger has no consumers yet, so the shadow data can be dropped and re-backfilled.\n142\t- **Exit check:**\n143\t  - V5 passes\n144\t  - RTO ≤ 4 h shown in the drill\n145\t  - p99 within the latency budget at 10×\n146\t  - rebuilding the state from the inbox gives exactly the live state\n147\t\n148\t**Phase 4: Cutover by cohort, then decommission**\n149\t- **Unlocks:** Ledger becomes the system of record.\n150\t- **Depends on:** V5.\n151\t- **Tasks:**\n152\t  1. Product and support read balances from Ledger (read-only credentials) behind a per-cohort flag: internal accounts → 5 % → 25 % → 100 %. Each step needs a clean daily reconciliation.\n153\t  2. The spreadsheet becomes read-only.\n154\t  3. Run V6, then retire the nightly script.\n155\t- **Rollback:** flip the cohort flag back to the spreadsheet path. **The point of no return is retiring the script, guarded by V6.** The spreadsheet is archived, not deleted.\n156\t- **Exit check:** V6 passes.\n157\t\n158\t## 6. Classification and constraints\n- **What:** A Python (standard library only) `LedgerService` that verifies PayCo webhooks and keeps per-account balances and entries from `payment.succeeded` and `refund.succeeded`. It replaces a nightly CSV-to-spreadsheet script and manual balance fixes.\n- **Type:** Software. Brownfield: the spreadsheet is the system of record today. Not a small build, because it handles money and takes input from an outside party.\n- **Dominant constraint:** Correctness. Balances must match PayCo's statement exactly, to the minor unit.\n- **Worst failure:** An account gets credit PayCo never collected, through a forged event or a payment applied twice after a duplicate delivery or a partial write. The customer spends it, and nobody notices until the monthly reconciliation.\n\n**Budgets**\n\n| Budget | Target | Label | Checked by |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| Correctness | 0 minor-unit difference per account vs PayCo statement | REQUIREMENT (brief: Finance reconciliation) | V3, V5 |\n| Replay window | Reject if \\|now − t\\| > 300 s | REQUIREMENT (PayCo integration guide) | V2, V4 |\n| RPO for acknowledged events | 0: no 2xx until the outcome is committed durably | REQUIREMENT (derived: PayCo treats any 2xx as delivered and will not reliably resend) | V1 |\n| RTO | ≤ 4 h | ASSUMPTION. PayCo retries for 72 h (REQUIREMENT ceiling), so 4 h leaves about 68 h of headroom | Phase 3 restore drill |\n| Latency | p99 `handle` below PayCo's delivery timeout | UNKNOWN: PayCo's timeout (PayCo docs or support; needed in Phase 2 for the sync/async row). Interim p99 ≤ 1 s (ASSUMPTION) | V0 baseline, Phase 3 exit |\n| Throughput | ~5k events/day now; design check at 10× (50k/day) | ASSUMPTION (brief says \"a few thousand\"; confirm against PayCo export counts in Phase 0). Growth rate UNKNOWN (product owner) | Phase 3 exit |\n| Drift detection window | ≤ 1 day (monthly today) | ASSUMPTION (a daily diff against PayCo's daily export is cheap) | Phase 3, V5 |\n| Storage | ≤ 10 MB/day of raw event bodies | ASSUMPTION (~1–2 KB/event × 5k) | Phase 3 |\n| Operational complexity | One process plus the managed DB; no queue, no cache | ASSUMPTION (team size not given) | Design |\n\n**Missing inputs** (the ones that would change the plan most):\n1. **ledgerkit README / MemoryStore API.** It is not in the workspace. I need to know whether the store has put-if-absent, compare-and-set, multi-key transactions or prefix scans, and whether a `StoreError` can be raised *after* the write took effect. These answers decide the data model (V1). The dev lead supplies them before Phase 1.\n2. **Does Ledger also record spends?** I assume not: Ledger holds funded credit (payments minus refunds), and spending is recorded elsewhere. If Ledger must take debits, a debit API and its contract become a new phase before Phase 3.\n3. **PayCo facts:** whether retries are re-signed with a fresh `t` (V4), the delivery timeout, whether a test mode exists, which fields the historical export has, and whether secret rotation is supported.\n4. **Finance facts:** the statement format (gross or net, fees), what the manual spreadsheet fixes represent, and how unhandled event types (disputes) should be treated in reconciliation. These are needed by Phase 3.\n5. **Named people:** a security reviewer (V2), the Finance approver (V5, V6), and the on-call owner for incident alerts.\n6. **The meaning of \"first payment\"** for the currency rule. I assume the first payment *applied*, not the earliest by `created`.\n7. **What depends on the spreadsheet.** The nightly script and support staff are CONFIRMED by the brief. That the product reads balances from the spreadsheet is ASSUMED; Phase 0 confirms it.\n\n## 2. Tradeoff gates (resolved up front)\n\n| Decision | Reversibility | Default (chosen now) | Assumption | Validated by | Flip condition |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| Consistency vs availability | R3: a balance that is wrong even briefly can be spent, and that loss is not recoverable | Consistent: no 2xx until committed; **one conditional write is the commit point** for each event's outcome; balance is the sum of entries; every verified raw body is kept in an inbox keyed by event `id` | The store offers put-if-absent or an atomic multi-key write; Then commit and respond.\n  6. Map any `StoreError` to 503 with no partial effect visible.\n  7. `balance` is the sum of committed entries, 0 for an unknown account; `entries` is in commit order, `[]` for an unknown account.\n  8. Unknown types are stored and return 200 `ignored`.\n- **Rollback:** shadow data only. Wipe the namespace and redeploy the Phase 0 artifact.\n- **Exit check:** V1 passes. An unsigned request carrying an existing `id` gets 401 and never touches the inbox, so nobody can plant a fake body under a real event `id`.\n\n**Phase 2: Money semantics**\n- **Unlocks:** correct balances for both handled types; readiness for live traffic.\n- **Depends on:** Phase 1, V1.\n- **Tasks:**\n  1. Strict validation:\n     - `amount` is an `int`, not a `bool` or `float`, and > 0\n     - `currency` matches `^[A-Z]{3}$`\n     - ids are non-empty strings; `created` is an `int`\n     - a signed body that fails validation goes to quarantine\n  2. Currency is fixed by the first applied payment; a payment in another currency goes to quarantine.\n  3. Refund handling:\n     - payment not yet seen → park\n     - payment seen → check account, amount and currency, allow one refund per `payment_id`, then apply a negative entry\n     - otherwise → quarantine\n     - a refund of a quarantined payment → quarantine\n  4. Release parked refunds when the payment commits (roll forward if interrupted), plus a timed sweep; alert when a refund has been parked longer than 96 h.\n  5. Reprocess tool: re-runs ignored or quarantined inbox events after a code fix, idempotently.\n  6. Parse multiple `v1` values in the signature header.\n  7. Run V3, run V2 and get the reviewer's sign-off, and finish V4 (it started in Phase 0).\n- **Rollback:** redeploy the Phase 1 artifact; rebuild the shadow state from the inbox.\n- **Exit check:** V2, V3 and V4 pass.\n\n**Phase 3: Live shadow, backfill and reconciliation** (system of record is still the spreadsheet; data flows one way, PayCo → Ledger)\n- **Unlocks:** evidence for the cutover.\n- **Depends on:** V2, V3, V4; the PayCo export; the Finance statement sample.\n- **Tasks:**\n  1. Register the live endpoint with a live secret.\n  2. Run the backfill from PayCo's export, keyed by `payment_id`, so it is idempotent and webhook duplicates of the same events collapse onto it.\n  3. Daily reconciliation job: Ledger vs PayCo's daily export (the one the nightly script already pulls) and vs the spreadsheet, per account, with differences sorted into categories.\n  4. Monthly reconciliation against the statement, with Finance.\n  5. Restore drill to measure RTO.\n  6. Replay 10× volume from the inbox against a staging copy to check latency and throughput.\n  7. Scan the stored inbox for personal data.\n  8. Run V5.\n- **Rollback:** disable the live endpoint. Ledger has no consumers yet, so the shadow data can be dropped and re-backfilled.\n- **Exit check:**\n  - V5 passes\n  - RTO ≤ 4 h shown in the drill\n  - p99 within the latency budget at 10×\n  - rebuilding the state from the inbox gives exactly the live state\n\n**Phase 4: Cutover by cohort, then decommission**\n- **Unlocks:** Ledger becomes the system of record.\n- **Depends on:** V5.\n- **Tasks:**\n  1. Product and support read balances from Ledger (read-only credentials) behind a per-cohort flag: internal accounts → 5 % → 25 % → 100 %. Each step needs a clean daily reconciliation.\n  2. The spreadsheet becomes read-only.\n  3. Run V6, then retire the nightly script.\n- **Rollback:** flip the cohort flag back to the spreadsheet path. **The point of no return is retiring the script, guarded by V6.** The spreadsheet is archived, not deleted.\n- **Exit check:** V6 passes.\n\n## 6. Validation gates\n\n| ID | Hypothesis | Method | Acceptance threshold | Evidence | Unlocks (and if it fails) | Phase |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| V0 | A change can be deployed, seen in logs and alerts, and rolled back across every tier in production | A test-mode event end to end; deploy and roll back through CI; inject a `StoreError` through a fault flag | One trace across PayCo → ingress → adapter → service → DB; a deploy and a rollback succeed; the injected `StoreError` gives 503, the alert fires, and PayCo's retry applies the event once; first latency and skew values recorded as BASELINE (a floor at near-zero load) | CI run log, a trace export and the alert record, kept in the repo `evidence/v0/` | Phase 1. If it fails: fix the pipeline before any further work | 0 |\n| V1 | A single conditional write can be the commit point, so any `StoreError` leaves the event either not applied or recoverable by retry | `FaultyStore` exhaustive injection: every write index × {fails before the write, fails after the write} × 6 scenarios, retried until 2xx | 0 deviations from the fault-free final state (balances, entries, outcomes): REQUIREMENT (exact match) | Test report plus the scenario scripts in `evidence/v1/` | Hardening the data model, then Phases 2 and 3. If it fails: the data-model flip in §3 | 1 |\n| V2 | Only authentic, fresh, consistent deliveries can change state | Vector suite: 1-byte body tamper, `t` tamper, wrong secret, `t` = now ± 300 accepted and ± 301 rejected, missing or malformed header, header-case variants, several `v1` values with one valid, unsigned request carrying an existing `id`, same `id` with a different signed body; code review for `compare_digest` and verify-before-parse; a check that logs contain no secret, body or signature | 100 % of vectors pass; the 409 leaves balances and entries byte-identical; the named security reviewer signs off (name UNKNOWN; the engineering lead supplies it in Phase 0) | Vector suite results plus the signed review in `evidence/v2/` | Live traffic in Phase 3. If it fails: no live registration | 2 |\n| V3 | Final state does not depend on delivery order, duplicates or `StoreError` | Seeded property test: random histories (multiple accounts and currencies, refunds, unknown types, conflicts, anomalies), shuffled, each event delivered 1–4 times including after a 2xx, random `StoreError`s, retried until 2xx; compared against an independent oracle | 0 mismatches over 10,000 histories (REQUIREMENT for 0; 10,000 is a design parameter); invariants hold: balance = sum of entries, ≤ 1 entry per `id`, ≤ 1 payment and ≤ 1 refund per `payment_id` | Run report plus failing seeds in `evidence/v3/` | Phase 3. If it fails: fix it and re-run before any live traffic | 2 |\n| V4 | PayCo re-signs every retry with a fresh `t` | In test mode, return 503 for one event across retries that span more than 300 s; record the header `t` against our receive clock | Every retry's `t` is within 300 s of when we received it (REQUIREMENT ±300 s from the PayCo guide) | Captured delivery log in `evidence/v4/` | The final tolerance design and Phase 3. If it fails: the tolerance flip in §3 | Starts in 0, required before 3 |\n| V5 | Ledger matches PayCo exactly on live data, backfill included | Daily per-account diff against PayCo's daily export for 30 consecutive days, plus one full monthly statement reconciled with Finance | 0 unexplained minor-unit differences (REQUIREMENT). The only allowed explanation is events of unhandled types listed from the inbox, signed off by the Finance approver (name UNKNOWN; Finance supplies it in Phase 0) | Daily diff reports plus the signed monthly reconciliation, kept in Finance-restricted storage | Phase 4. If it fails: stay in shadow, fix, restart the 30-day count; if disputes cause the differences, bring dispute handling forward | 3 |\n| V6 | The nightly script can be retired safely | Rehearsal: one monthly close with Ledger as system of record and the script running read-only alongside; restore the script from the archive once | Go/no-go: 0 differences at that close, no quarantined events older than 7 days, restore of the script tested; the Finance approver signs | Rehearsal report, restore log and the signed go/no-go in Finance-restricted storage | Retiring the script. If it fails: keep the script and repeat next month | 4 |\n\n## 7. Cross-cutting concerns\n\n| Phase | Security | Observability | Reproducibility | Resilience |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| 0 | Secret held in a secret manager; signature and time check before parsing; shadow namespace; nothing reads the data | JSON log line per delivery (`event_id`, type, outcome, status, latency, skew) tagged as internal financial data; alerts on 5xx, 401 and `StoreError` | Pinned Python version, standard library only, versioned CI artifact; injected clock; signed test fixtures using a synthetic secret | `StoreError` returns 503 so PayCo retries; endpoint kill switch in PayCo |\n| 1 | 409 for a body conflict plus an incident record and a page; unsigned requests never write to the inbox | Counters per outcome (applied, duplicate, ignored, conflict); a roll-forward recovery counter | `FaultyStore` and the V1 scenarios checked in and deterministic | A single commit write; roll forward on retry; a `StoreError` is treated as an unknown outcome |\n| 2 | Strict schema (rejects `bool` and `float` amounts); multiple `v1` values; V2 review | Count and age gauges for quarantined and parked events, with alerts | Property-test seeds recorded; reprocess tool is deterministic | Parked-refund sweep; reprocess quarantined events after a fix |\n| 3 | Live secret issued; inbox access limited to Finance and on-call; personal-data scan | Daily reconciliation report with an alert on any difference; alert when no live delivery arrives for 6 h | Idempotent, rerunnable backfill; rebuild from the inbox equals live state | Restore drill (RTO); load check at 10× |\n| 4 | Read-only DB credentials for product and support; balances no longer edited by hand | Per-cohort diff of Ledger vs spreadsheet; support dashboard | Cohort flag configuration under version control | Per-cohort flag rollback; nightly script archived and restorable until V6 |\n\n## 8. AI layer\nN/A: no AI component.\n\n## 9. Methodology exceptions\nNone.\n\n## 10. Deliberately deferred\n- **Handlers for disputes, payouts and other types.** The inbox already keeps them for replay. Header names are matched case-insensitively.\n- **Idempotency:** inbox keyed by event `id` holds the canonical-JSON SHA-256 (sorted keys,\n  compact), the raw-bytes hash, and the raw body (base64). Same id + same canonical body → 200\n  `{\"status\":\"duplicate\",\"outcome\":<stored outcome>}`, no state change. Same id + different body →\n  409, an `incidents` row is written, accounts/entries untouched.\n  If the incident write hits a StoreError the response is 503 (not 409), so the retry records it.\n- **Unknown types:** verified, stored in the inbox, 200 `ignored`.\n- **Validation (handled types):** `data` object; non-empty string `account_id`/`payment_id`;\n  `amount` int (not bool/float) > 0; `currency` matches `[A-Z]{3}`; `created` int. A signed body\n  that fails validation (or has a missing/non-string type) with a usable `id` is stored and answered\n  200 `quarantined` (never applied). Non-JSON bodies, non-object bodies, or a missing `id` → 400\n  (no key to store under).\n- **Currency:** fixed by the first *applied* payment on the account; a payment in another currency\n  is quarantined.\n- **Payments:** a second event with an already-applied `payment_id` is quarantined.\n- **Refunds:** payment unknown → parked (200 `parked`, no entry). When the payment is applied in a\n  later delivery, parked refunds are released in that same transaction (checked for account, amount,\n  currency; at most one refund per `payment_id`) and the inbox outcome becomes `applied` (or\n  `quarantined`). A redelivery of a parked refund also re-evaluates it. Refund of a quarantined\n  payment → quarantined. Mismatch or second refund → quarantined.\n- **Entries/balance:** append-only entries keyed `<quoted account>/<seq>`; `balance` is the sum of\n  entries (0 if unknown); `entries` in commit order (`[]` if unknown), each with `event_id`, `type`,\n  `amount` (signed), plus `currency`, `payment_id`, `created`, `account_id`, `seq`. A refund entry\n  appears when applied (after its payment), not when received.\n- Balances can go negative only if a refund is applied after the credit was spent elsewhere; Ledger\n  does not track spends (PLAN missing input 2 assumption).\n\n## Assumptions / open items\n- PayCo re-signs retries with a fresh `t` (PLAN V4, unvalidated). A retry with a stale `t` would be\n  rejected 401 per the brief/guide.\n- Single process/writer; the store is single-threaded, no locking added.\n- A parked refund whose payment never arrives stays parked (no timer/alert in this codebase).\n- Quarantine is only visible in the inbox table (`outcome == \"quarantined\"`, `reason`); paging on it\n  is for the ops layer.\n","numLines":53,"startLine":1,"totalLines":53}}}

_refund_verdict(self, txn, f):\n        \"\"\"('apply'|'park'|'quarantine'|'reject', reason) for a valid refund, given current state.\n\n        Partial refunds: several refunds may apply to one payment as long as their total never\n        exceeds the payment's amount; a refund that would exceed it is 'reject' (HTTP 422).\"\"\"\n        payment = txn.get(\"payments\", f[\"payment_id\"])\n \"park\", None\n        if payment[\"account_id\"] != f[\"account_id\"] or payment[\"currency\"] != f[\"currency\"]:\n            return \"quarantine\", \"refund does not match its payment\"\n        return self._cap_verdict(txn, payment, f)\n\n    def _refunded_total(self, txn, payment):\n        row = txn.get(\"refunds\", payment[\"payment_id\"])\n        if row is None:\n            return 0\n        # Rows written before partial refunds existed had no \"total\"; those were full refunds.\n        return row[\"total\"] if \"total\" in row else payment[\"amount\"]\n\n    def _cap_verdict(self, txn, payment, f):\n        if self._refunded_total(txn, payment) + f[\"amount\"] > payment[\"amount\"]:\n            return \"reject\", \"refund would exceed the payment amount\"\n        return \"apply\", None\n\n    def _apply_refund_entry(self, txn, f):\n        payment = txn.get(\"payments\", f[\"payment_id\"])\n        total = self._refunded_total(txn, payment) + f[\"amount\"]\n        self._append_entry(txn, f, -f[\"amount\"], f[\"currency\"])\n        txn.put(\"refunds\", f[\"payment_id\"], {\"total\": total, \"last_event_id\": f[\"event_id\"]})\n\n    def _apply_refund(self, txn, eid, record, f):\n        verdict, reason = self._refund_verdict(txn, f)\n        if verdict == \"park\":\n            record[\"fields\"] = f\n            txn.put(\"parked\", \"%s/%s\" % (_q(f[\"payment_id\"]), _q(eid)), f)\n            return self._finish(txn, eid, record, \"parked\")\n        if verdict == \"quarantine\":\n            return self._finish(txn, eid, record, \"quarantined\", reason)\n        if verdict == \"reject\":\n            # Over-refund: no entry, no change to the refunded total. _refund_verdict(self, txn, f):\n        \"\"\"('apply'|'park'|'quarantine'|'reject', reason) for a valid refund, given current state.\n\n        Partial refunds: several refunds may apply to one payment as long as their total never\n        exceeds the payment's amount; a refund that would exceed it is 'reject' (HTTP 422).\"\"\"\n        payment = txn.get(\"payments\", f[\"payment_id\"])\n        if payment is None:\n            if txn.get(\"bad_payments\", f[\"payment_id\"]) is not None:\n                return \"quarantine\", \"refund of a quarantined payment\"\n            return       # Rows written before partial refunds existed had no \"total\"; those were full refunds.\n        return row[\"total\"] if \"total\" in row else payment[\"amount\"]\n\n    def _cap_verdict(self, txn, payment, f):\n        if self._refunded_total(txn, payment) + f[\"amount\"] > payment[\"amount\"]:\n            return \"reject\", \"refund would exceed the payment amount\"\n        return self._append_entry(txn, f, -f[\"amount\"], f[\"currency\"])\n        txn.put(\"refunds\", f[\"payment_id\"], {\"total\": total, \"last_event_id\": f[\"event_id\"]})\n\n    def _apply_refund(self, txn, eid, record, f):\n        verdict, reason = self._refund_verdict(txn, f)\n        if verdict == \"park\":\n            record[\"fields\"] = f\n            txn.put(\"parked\", \"%s/%s\" % (_q(f[\"payment_id\"]), _q(eid)), f)\n            return self._finish(txn, eid, record, \"parked\")\n        if verdict == \"quarantine\":\n            return self._finish(txn, eid, record, \"quarantined\", reason)\n        if verdict == \"reject\":\n            # Over-refund: no entry, no change to the refunded total. The inbox row keeps the\n            # evidence for finance; the delivery is answered 422.\n            record[\"fields\"] = f\n            return self._finish(txn, eid, record, \"rejected\", reason)\n        self._apply_refund_entry(txn, f)\n        return self._finish(txn, eid, record, \"applied\")\n\n    def _settle_parked_refund(self, txn, f, record):\n        \"\"\"Re-evaluate a parked (or previously rejected) refund already in the inbox; update its\n        outcome. A rejected refund stays rejected unless the state now lets it apply.\"\"\"\n        was_parked = record[\"outcome\"] == \"parked\"\n        verdict, reason = self._refund_verdict(txn, f)\n        if verdict == \"park\":\n            return\n        if verdict == \"reject\" and not was_parked:\n            return  # still rejected; nothing to write\n        if verdict == \"apply\":\n            self._apply_refund_entry(txn, f)\n            record[\"outcome\"], record[\"reason\"] = \"applied\", None\n        elif verdict == \"reject\":\n            record[\"outcome\"], record[\"reason\"] = \"rejected\", reason\n        else:\n            record[\"outcome\"], record[\"reason\"] = \"quarantined\", reason\n        txn.put(\"inbox\", f[\"event_id\"], record)\n        if was_parked:\n            txn.delete(\"parked\", \"%s/%s\" % (_q(f[\"payment_id\"]), _q(f[\"event_id\"])))\n\n    def _release_parked(self, txn, payment_id):\n        # Deterministic order (event time, then id) so the outcome does not depend on key order.\n        parked = txn.scan(\"parked\", _q(payment_id) + \"/\")\n        parked.sort(key=lambda kv: (kv[1][\"created\"], kv[1][\"event_id\"]))\n        for _key, f in parked:\n            record = txn.get(\"inbox\", f[\"event_id\"])\n            if record is not None and record[\"outcome\"] == \"parked\":\n                self._settle_parked_refund(txn, f, record)\n            else:\n                txn.delete(\"parked\", _key)","originalFile":"\"\"\"Ledger service: ingests PayCo webhook deliveries and keeps account balances. See BRIEF.md.\n\nDesign (details in NOTES.md): every delivery is handled in ONE store transaction, so the inbox\nrecord, the outcome and the ledger entries commit together or not at all. A StoreError rolls the\nwhole transaction back and is answered with 503, so PayCo's retry re-does the event cleanly.\n\nTables:\n  inbox        event id -> {hash, raw, type, outcome, reason, ...}        if isinstance(key, bytes):\n                key = key.decode(\"latin-1\")\n            if isinstance(key, str) and key.strip().lower() == name:\n                if isinstance(value, bytes):\n _validate(self, event):\n        data = event.get(\"data\")\n        if not isinstance(data, dict):\n            return None, \"data is not an object\"\n        created = event.get(\"created\")\n        if not _is_int(created):\n            return None, \"created is not an integer\"\n        for name in (\"account_id\", \"payment_id\"):\n            if not _is_id(data.get(name)):\n integer\"\n        currency = data.get(\"currency\")\n        if not isinstance(currency, str) or not _CURRENCY_RE.fullmatch(currency):\n            return None, \"currency is not an ISO 4217 code\"\n         \"amount\": amount, \"currency\": currency,\n        }, None\n\n    def _finish(self, txn, eid, record, outcome, reason=None):\n        record[\"outcome\"] = outcome\n        record[\"reason\"] = reason\n        txn.put(\"inbox\", eid, record)\n        body = {\"status\": outcome, \"event_id\": eid}\n        if outcome == \"rejected\":\n            body[\"error\"] = for a valid refund, given current state.\n\n        Partial refunds: several refunds may apply to one payment as long as their total never\n        exceeds the payment's amount; a refund that would exceed it is 'reject' (HTTP 422).\"\"\"\n        payment = txn.get(\"payments\", f[\"payment_id\"])\n        if payment is None:\n            if txn.get(\"bad_payments\", f[\"payment_id\"]) is not payment):\n        row = txn.get(\"refunds\", payment_id)\n        if row is None:\n            return 0\n        # Rows written before partial refunds existed had no \"total\"; those were full refunds.\n        return row[\"total\"] if \"total\" in row else payment[\"amount\"]\n\n    def _cap_verdict(self, txn, payment, f):\n        if self._refunded_total(txn, f[\"payment_id\"], payment) + The inbox row keeps the\n            # evidence for finance; the delivery is answered 422.\n            record[\"fields\"] = f\n            return self._finish(txn, eid, record, \"rejected\", reason)\n        self._apply_refund_entry(txn, f)\n        return self._finish(txn, eid, record, \"applied\")\n\n    def _settle_parked_refund(self, txn, f, record):\n        \"\"\"Re-evaluate a parked (or previously rejected) refund already in the inbox; update its\n        outcome. A rejected refund stays rejected unless the state now lets it apply.\"\"\"\n        was_parked = record[\"outcome\"] == \"parked\"\n        verdict, reason = self._refund_verdict(txn, f)\n        if verdict == \"park\":\n            return\n        if verdict == \"reject\" and not was_parked:\n            return  # still rejected; nothing to write\n        if verdict == \"apply\":\n            self._apply_refund_entry(txn, f)\n            record[\"outcome\"], record[\"reason\"] = \"applied\", None\n        elif verdict == \"reject\":\n            record[\"outcome\"], record[\"reason\"] = \"rejected\", reason\n        else:\n            record[\"outcome\"], record[\"reason\"] = \"quarantined\", reason\n        txn.put(\"inbox\", f[\"event_id\"], record)\n        if was_parked:\n            txn.delete(\"parked\", \"%s/%s\" % (_q(f[\"payment_id\"]), _q(f[\"event_id\"])))\n\n    def _release_parked(self, txn, payment_id):\n        # Deterministic order (event time, then id) so the outcome does not depend on key order.\n        parked = txn.scan(\"parked\", _q(payment_id) + \"/\")\n        parked.sort(key=lambda kv: (kv[1][\"created\"], kv[1][\"event_id\"]))\n        for _key, f in parked:\n            record = txn.get(\"inbox\", f[\"event_id\"])\n            if record is not None and record[\"outcome\"] == \"parked\":\n                self._settle_parked_refund(txn, f, record)\n            else:\n                txn.delete(\"parked\", _key)\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":9,"oldLines":7,"newStart":9,"newLines":7,"lines":["   accounts     account id -> {currency, seq}","   entries      \"<quoted account>/<seq>\" -> entry dict                (append-only)","   payments     payment id -> {event_id, account_id, amount, currency}  (applied payments)","-  refunds      payment id -> {event_id}                               (applied refunds)","+  refunds      payment id -> {total, last_event_id}                   (total refunded so far)","   bad_payments payment id -> {event_id}                               (quarantined payments)","   parked       \"<quoted payment>/<quoted event>\" -> refund fields     (refunds awaiting payment)","   incidents    \"<event id>/<hash>\" -> {...}                           (same id, different body)"]}],"userModified":false,"replaceAll":false}}

On release (payment commit, or a","+  redelivery of the parked refund) refunds are evaluated in (`created`, event id) order so the","+  outcome does not depend on key order. If a released refund would exceed the payment, its inbox","+  outcome becomes `rejected` (no entry); the delivery that triggered the release (the payment, or","+  a redelivery) is not failed by it, and PayCo's later redelivery of that refund gets the 422.","+  Since the original refund delivery was already answered 200, the inbox is the only signal.","+- **Order dependence.** Which refunds get rejected can depend on arrival order when their sum","+  exceeds the payment (e.g. 60, 50, 40 on a 100 payment: 60 and 40 apply, 50 is rejected; in","+  another order a different one is). Every accepted state is still ≤ the payment amount, which is","+  what the request requires; finance investigates rejections.","+- **Idempotency** is by event `id` only. Two different events refunding the same amount of the","+  same payment are two refunds (they are distinguishable only by `id`), bounded by the cap.","+- **Storage.** `refunds` rows are now `{total, last_event_id}`. Legacy rows (`{event_id}` only, from","+  full refunds) are read as total = payment amount.","+- Open question for finance: should a rejected refund ever be force-applied or reversed? Not","+  handled; it needs a manual-adjustment path (PLAN §10).","+"," ## Assumptions / open items"," - PayCo re-signs retries with a fresh `t` (PLAN V4, unvalidated). A retry with a stale `t` would be","   rejected 401 per the brief/guide."]}],"userModified":false,"replaceAll":false}}

One payment can have several refunds. I removed the old rules that a refund must equal the payment amount and that a payment gets only one refund.\n- **Over-refunds:** a refund that would push the total past the payment amount gets HTTP 422 with no entry, so the balance doesn't change. The delivery is still saved in the inbox with outcome `rejected`, so finance can find it. A same-id, different-body redelivery is still caught as a 409. A redelivery of the rejected refund gets 422 again.\n- **Refund before payment:** these are still parked with a 200. When they are released after the payment arrives, they are processed in `created` order, then event id. If a released refund would exceed the payment, its inbox outcome becomes `rejected`. The original 200 can't be taken back, so the inbox is the only signal until PayCo redelivers and gets the 422.\n- **Unchanged:** an account or currency mismatch, or a refund of a quarantined payment, is still quarantined with a 200. Signatures, idempotency by event `id`, the single transaction per delivery, and `StoreError` giving 503 are also unchanged.\n- **Stored data:** refund totals are now stored per payment as `{total, last_event_id}`. Old rows from full refunds are read as the payment amount.\n- **Arrival order:** if several refunds together exceed a payment, which ones get rejected depends on arrival order. Every accepted state stays within the payment amount. PayCo will also keep retrying a 422 for up to 72 hours.\n\n`NOTES.md` also records that `PLAN.md` §10 deferred partial refunds and that this change pulls them forward.
